class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
                undefinedFunction(word)
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
                undefinedFunction(word)
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

But original algorithm with j as exclusive boundary and checking prefix from i to j-1: We can implement:

def first_forbidden_length(i, j):
    t = trie
    limit = min(j, i + max_len)
    for pos in range(i, limit):
        c = word[pos]
        if c not in t:
            return 0
        t = t[c]
        if "end" in t:
            return pos - i + 1
    return 0

Then main:
n = len(word)
j = n
res = 0
for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
    truc = first_forbidden_length(i, j)
    if truc:
        j = i + truc - 1
    length = j - i
    if length > res: res = length
return res

Need ensure code is syntactically correct. Let's craft:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        n = len(word)
        max_len = max((len(f) for f in forbidden), default=0)

        def forbidden_prefix_len(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for pos in range(i, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = n
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            l = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if l:
                j = i + l - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans

Let's craft final code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_len(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for pos in range(i, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = n
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            blocked = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if blocked:
                j = i + blocked - 1
            if j - i > ans:
                ans = j - i
        return ans

Let's maybe formalize proof in mind:
Define f(i) = max exclusive end e such that word[i:e] valid. We process i descending. Suppose we know f(i+1) = j. For any e > j, word[i+1:e] invalid (since e > f(i+1)), and word[i+1:e] is a substring of word[i:e], so word[i:e] invalid. Therefore f(i) <= j. Now consider word[i:j]. If it contains no forbidden substring, then f(i)=j. If it contains a forbidden substring, since word[i+1:j] is valid, the forbidden substring must start at i. Let l be the minimum length of a forbidden prefix of word[i:j]. Then any e >= i+l makes word[i:e] invalid, and e = i+l-1 makes word[i:e] valid (because it is shorter than the shortest forbidden prefix, and word[i+1:e] is valid). Thus f(i)=i+l-1. Our algorithm computes exactly that. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a known LeetCode solution that uses a set and sliding window:
```
forbidden = set(forbidden)
left = 0
ans = 0
for right in range(len(word)):
    for i in range(max(left, right-10), right+1):
        if word[i:right+1] in forbidden:
            left = i+1
    ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
```
This is simpler and O(n*10). Our trie algorithm is also correct. But wait, our algorithm processes starts from right to left; it returns longest valid substring. Let's compare with sliding window on a tricky case where a forbidden word appears and left moves. Both should match.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_len(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for pos in range(i, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = n

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            blocked = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if blocked:
                j = i + blocked - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0

        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        n = len(word)

        def forbidden_prefix_len(i: int, j: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(j, i + max_len)
            for pos in range(i, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - i + 1
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = n

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            blocked = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if blocked:
                j = i + blocked - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
